**Charles III** (Charles Philip Arthur George; born 14 November 1948) is [King of the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_the_United_Kingdom) and 14 other [Commonwealth realms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_realm).[^cite_note-realms-3]

Charles is the eldest son of [Queen Elizabeth II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_II) and [Prince Philip, Duke of Edinburgh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Philip,_Duke_of_Edinburgh). He was born during the reign of his maternal grandfather, [King George VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_George_VI), and became [heir apparent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heir_apparent) following his mother's accession in 1952. Charles was created [Prince of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Wales) in 1958 and [his investiture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investiture_of_Charles,_Prince_of_Wales) was held in 1969. He was educated at [Cheam School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheam_School) and [Gordonstoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gordonstoun), and later spent six months at the [Timbertop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timbertop) campus of [Geelong Grammar School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geelong_Grammar_School) in Victoria, Australia. After completing a history degree at the [University of Cambridge](25978572.md), he served in the [Royal Air Force](25679.md) and the [Royal Navy](26061.md) from 1971 to 1976. [He married](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wedding_of_Prince_Charles_and_Lady_Diana_Spencer) [Lady Diana Spencer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_Diana_Spencer) in 1981 and they had two sons, [William](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William,_Prince_of_Wales) in 1982 and [Harry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Harry,_Duke_of_Sussex) in 1984. Charles and Diana divorced in August 1996 after years of estrangement and well-publicised extramarital affairs. [Diana died](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Diana,_Princess_of_Wales) the following year from injuries sustained in a car crash. In 2005, [Charles married](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wedding_of_Prince_Charles_and_Camilla_Parker_Bowles) his long-time partner, [Camilla Parker Bowles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camilla_Parker_Bowles).

As heir apparent, Charles undertook official duties and engagements on behalf of his mother and represented the United Kingdom on visits abroad. He founded the [Prince's Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince's_Trust)[^cite_note-7] in 1976, sponsored [the Prince's Charities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince's_Charities) and became patron or president of more than 800 other charities and organisations. He advocated for the conservation of historic buildings and the importance of traditional architecture in society, and in that vein generated the experimental new town of [Poundbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poundbury). An [environmentalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmentalist), Charles supported [organic farming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organic_farming) and action to address [climate change](5042951.md) during his time as manager of the [Duchy of Cornwall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duchy_of_Cornwall) estates, [earning him awards and recognition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_awards_received_by_Charles_III). He has also been a prominent critic of [genetically modified food](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genetically_modified_food), and has drawn scrutiny for his support of [alternative medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_medicine). He has [authored or co-authored 17 books](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bibliography_of_Charles_III).

Charles became king upon [his mother's death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_state_funeral_of_Elizabeth_II) in 2022. At the age of 73, he was the oldest person to accede to the British throne, after having been the longest-serving heir apparent and Prince of Wales in British history. Significant events of his reign have included [his coronation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronation_of_Charles_III_and_Camilla) in 2023; his cancer diagnosis in 2024, which temporarily suspended planned public engagements; and the removal of his brother [Andrew](125267.md)'s remaining titles, styles, and honours in 2025.

## Early life, family, and education

An infant Charles in a white christening gown with his parents and grandparents

Christening of Charles (centre, wearing the [royal christening gown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_christening_gown)) in 1948: (from left to right) his grandfather King George VI; his mother, Princess Elizabeth, holding him; his father, Philip; and his grandmother [Queen Elizabeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_the_Queen_Mother)

Charles was born at 9\:14 pm on 14 November 1948[^cite_note-8] by [caesarean section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caesarean_section) at [Buckingham Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buckingham_Palace),[^cite_note-9] during the reign of his maternal grandfather, [King George VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_George_VI). He was the first child of Princess Elizabeth, Duchess of Edinburgh (later [Queen Elizabeth II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_II)), and [Philip, Duke of Edinburgh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip,_Duke_of_Edinburgh).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth2007120-10] He was [christened](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_baptism) Charles Philip Arthur George on 15 December in the Music Room at Buckingham Palace by the [archbishop of Canterbury](2345.md), [Geoffrey Fisher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffrey_Fisher).[^cite_note-13][^cite_note-15][^cite_note-PoWsite-16][^cite_note-17] He has three younger siblings: [Anne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anne,_Princess_Royal) (born 1950), [Andrew](125267.md) (born 1960), and [Edward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Edward,_Duke_of_Edinburgh) (born 1964).[^cite_note-18]

[George VI died](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_VI_died) on 6 February 1952, after which Charles's mother acceded to the throne as Elizabeth II and he became [heir apparent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heir_apparent). Under a charter issued by [Edward III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_III) in 1337, and as the monarch's eldest son, he automatically assumed the titles of [Duke of Cornwall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duke_of_Cornwall) and, in the Scottish peerage, [Duke of Rothesay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duke_of_Rothesay), [Earl of Carrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earl_of_Carrick), [Baron of Renfrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baron_of_Renfrew_\(title\)), [Lord of the Isles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lord_of_the_Isles), and [Prince and Great Steward of Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_and_Great_Steward_of_Scotland).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth2007127-19] He attended [his mother's coronation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronation_of_Elizabeth_II) at [Westminster Abbey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westminster_Abbey) on 2 June 1953.[^cite_note-20] He was later created [Prince of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Wales) in 1958.

When Charles was five, Catherine Peebles was appointed as his [governess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governess) to oversee his education at Buckingham Palace.[^cite_note-21] He began attending [Hill House School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hill_House_School) in [West London](60445975.md) in November 1956,[^cite_note-22] becoming the first heir apparent to be educated at a school rather than by private tutors.[^cite_note-time_1988-23] He did not receive preferential treatment from the school's founder and headmaster, [Stuart Townend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stuart_Townend_\(headmaster\)), who encouraged the Queen to have Charles train in [football](10568.md), noting that boys on the pitch were never deferential to anyone.[^cite_note-24] Charles later attended two of his father's former schools: [Cheam School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheam_School) in [Hampshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampshire)[^cite_note-debrett-25] from 1958,[^cite_note-schooling-26] followed by [Gordonstoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gordonstoun) in [Moray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moray) in Scotland, where he began classes in April 1962.[^cite_note-schooling-26][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth2007139-27] He became patron of Gordonstoun in May 2024.[^cite_note-28]

[Jonathan Dimbleby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jonathan_Dimbleby)'s [authorised 1994 biography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince_of_Wales:_A_Biography) described Elizabeth and Philip as physically and emotionally distant parents, and criticised Philip for disregarding Charles's sensitive nature, including insisting that Charles attend Gordonstoun, where he was bullied.[^cite_note-29] Although Charles reportedly referred to the school as "[Colditz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oflag_IV-C) in [kilts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kilt)",[^cite_note-debrett-25] he later praised Gordonstoun for teaching him "a great deal about myself and my own abilities and disabilities". In a 1975 interview Charles said he was "glad" to have attended the school and that its "toughness" had been "much exaggerated".[^cite_note-Colditz_kilts-30]

In 1966, Charles spent two terms at the [Timbertop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timbertop) campus of [Geelong Grammar School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geelong_Grammar_School) in Victoria, Australia, during which he visited Papua New Guinea on a school trip with his history tutor, Michael Collins Persse.[^cite_note-powedu-31][^cite_note-32] Whilst based at Timbertop, he frequently travelled to the main [Geelong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geelong) campus as part of his schooling arrangements, residing in Cuthbertson House, one of the school's boarding houses.[^cite_note-Pommie_Prince_rolls_back_39_years_with_a_return_visit_to_Timbertop-33][^cite_note-A_reunion_to_remember-34] Charles later described his time at Timbertop as the most enjoyable part of his education.[^cite_note-35] On returning to Gordonstoun, he became [head boy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_boy), and left in 1967 with six [GCE O-levels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GCE_O-levels) and two [A-levels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A-level) in history and French, at grades B and C respectively.[^cite_note-powedu-31][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth2007145-36] Reflecting on his schooling, Charles later remarked that he "didn't enjoy school as much as I might have", adding that he was "happier at home than anywhere else".[^cite_note-Colditz_kilts-30]

Breaking royal tradition, Charles proceeded directly to university after completing his A‑levels rather than joining the [British Armed Forces](31730.md).[^cite_note-debrett-25] In October 1967, he was admitted to the [University of Cambridge](25978572.md) to study [archaeology](18951655.md) and [anthropology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthropology) for the first part of the [Tripos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tripos) at [Trinity College, Cambridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinity_College,_Cambridge), later switching to [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faculty_of_History,_University_of_Cambridge).[^cite_note-PoWsite-16][^cite_note-powedu-31][^cite_note-37] During his second year he spent one term at the [University College of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_College_of_Wales) in [Aberystwyth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aberystwyth), studying [Welsh history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welsh_history) and [Welsh language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welsh_language).[^cite_note-powedu-31] Charles became the first British heir apparent to earn a university degree, graduating from Cambridge in June 1970 with a Bachelor of Arts (BA) degree with [lower second-class honours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_second-class_honours) (2\:2).[^cite_note-powedu-31][^cite_note-38] Following standard practice, his BA was promoted to a [Master of Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Master_of_Arts_\(Oxbridge_and_Dublin\)) (MA Cantab) in August 1975.[^cite_note-powedu-31]

## Prince of Wales

Charles in a suit sits on stage with a book, surrounded by others in similar attire. A choir is visible in the background.

Charles at the Flint National Eisteddfod, several days after his [investiture as the Prince of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investiture_of_Charles,_Prince_of_Wales).

Charles was created [Prince of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Wales) and [Earl of Chester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earl_of_Chester) on 26 July 1958,[^cite_note-39] although [his investiture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investiture_of_Charles,_Prince_of_Wales) did not take place until 1 July 1969, when he was crowned by his mother in a televised ceremony at [Caernarfon Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caernarfon_Castle).[^cite_note-40] The [event was controversial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controversy_of_the_Prince_of_Wales_title) in Wales amid rising [Welsh nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welsh_nationalist) sentiment.[^cite_note-41] He [took his seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Introduction_\(House_of_Lords\)) in the [House of Lords](13658.md) the following year,[^cite_note-42] delivering his [maiden speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maiden_speech) on 13 June 1974,[^cite_note-43] the first royal to speak from the floor since the future [Edward VII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_VII) in 1884.[^cite_note-44] He addressed the House again in 1975.[^cite_note-45]

Charles increasingly undertook public duties, founding [the Prince's Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince's_Trust) in 1976[^cite_note-46] and travelling to the United States in 1981.[^cite_note-47] In the mid-1970s he expressed interest in serving as [Governor-General of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor-General_of_Australia), following a suggestion by the [Australian prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_prime_minister), [Malcolm Fraser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malcolm_Fraser). The proposal was ultimately abandoned owing to a lack of public enthusiasm.[^cite_note-48] Charles later remarked, "so, what are you supposed to think when you are prepared to do something to help and you are just told you're not wanted?"[^cite_note-49]

### Military training and career

Charles served in the [Royal Air Force](25679.md) (RAF) and the [Royal Navy](26061.md) during the 1970s. His military training began in 1969, in his second year at Cambridge, when he joined the [Cambridge University Air Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambridge_University_Air_Squadron) and learned to fly the [Chipmunk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Havilland_Canada_DHC-1_Chipmunk).[^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51] He was presented with his [RAF wings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aircrew_brevet) in August 1971.[^cite_note-52]

Three county-class destroyers sailing in the English Channel

(Front to back) [HMS *Norfolk*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Norfolk_\(D21\)), [*London*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_London_\(D16\)), and [*Antrim*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Antrim_\(D18\)) in the [English Channel](9230.md) following joint exercises with the RAF in December 1971. Charles was serving aboard *Norfolk* at this time.

After the [passing-out](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passing_out_\(military\)) parade that September, Charles embarked on a naval career and undertook a six-week course at the [Royal Naval College, Dartmouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Naval_College,_Dartmouth). He subsequently served from 1971 to 1972 on the guided-missile destroyer [HMS *Norfolk*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Norfolk_\(D21\)), and on the frigates [HMS *Minerva*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Minerva_\(F45\)) from 1972 to 1973 and [HMS *Jupiter*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Jupiter_\(F60\)) in 1974. That same year he qualified as a helicopter pilot at [RNAS Yeovilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RNAS_Yeovilton), and during his helicopter training completed commando instruction at the [Commando Training Centre Royal Marines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commando_Training_Centre_Royal_Marines) at Lympstone.[^cite_note-53] Charles then joined [845 Naval Air Squadron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/845_Naval_Air_Squadron), a [Royal Marines](93985.md) air support unit of the Fleet Air Arm, serving as a pilot aboard [HMS *Hermes*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Hermes_\(R12\)) and flying the Royal Marines commando variant of the [Westland Wessex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westland_Wessex#Variants) helicopter.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth2007170-54][^cite_note-55]

Charles spent his final ten months of active naval service commanding the coastal minehunter [HMS *Bronington*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Bronington), beginning on 9 February 1976.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth2007170-54] He retired from active service later that year with the rank of [Commander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander_\(Royal_Navy\)).[^cite_note-56] Two years later he undertook the parachute training course at [RAF Brize Norton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAF_Brize_Norton), having been appointed colonel-in-chief of the [Parachute Regiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parachute_Regiment_\(United_Kingdom\)) in 1977, and was a member of Parachute Course 841a.[^cite_note-57][^cite_note-58] Charles gave up flying after crash-landing a [BAe 146](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BAe_146) in [Islay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islay) in 1994, when, as a passenger invited to fly the aircraft, he was at the controls; a board of inquiry found the crew negligent.[^cite_note-59]

### Relationships and marriages

#### Bachelorhood

In his youth, Charles was romantically linked to several women. His girlfriends included Georgiana Russell, the daughter of [Sir John Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_John_Russell), who was the [British ambassador to Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_ambassador_to_Spain);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth2007192-60] [Lady Jane Wellesley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_Jane_Wellesley);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth2007193-61] Davina Sheffield;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth2007194-62] [Lady Sarah Spencer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_Sarah_Spencer);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth2007195-63] and [Camilla Shand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camilla_Shand), who later became his second wife.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth200715–17,_178-64]

Portrait of Charles seated

Photograph by [Allan Warren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allan_Warren), 1972

Charles's great-uncle [Lord Mountbatten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lord_Mountbatten) advised him to "sow his wild oats and have as many affairs as he can before settling down", but, for a wife, he "should choose a suitable, attractive, and sweet-charactered girl before she has met anyone else she might fall for ... It is disturbing for women to have experiences if they have to remain on a pedestal after marriage".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJunor200572-65] Early in 1974, Mountbatten began corresponding with 25-year-old Charles about a potential marriage to his granddaughter, [Amanda Knatchbull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amanda_Knatchbull).[^cite_note-66] Charles wrote to Amanda's mother, [Lady Brabourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patricia_Knatchbull,_2nd_Countess_Mountbatten_of_Burma), who was also his godmother, expressing interest in her daughter. Lady Brabourne replied approvingly, but suggested that a courtship with a 16-year-old was premature.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDimbleby1994263-67]

Four years later, Mountbatten arranged for Amanda and himself to accompany Charles on his 1980 visit to India. Both fathers, however, objected: Prince Philip feared that his famous uncle[^cite_note-68] would eclipse Charles, while [Lord Brabourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Knatchbull,_7th_Baron_Brabourne) warned that a joint visit would concentrate media attention on the cousins before they could decide whether to become a couple.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDimbleby1994263–265-69]

In August 1979, before Charles was due to depart alone for India, [Mountbatten was assassinated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Lord_Mountbatten) by the [Provisional Irish Republican Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provisional_Irish_Republican_Army). When Charles returned, he proposed to Amanda. But in addition to her grandfather, she had lost her [paternal grandmother](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doreen_Knatchbull,_Baroness_Brabourne) and [younger brother](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicholas_Knatchbull) in the bomb attack, and was now reluctant to join the royal family.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDimbleby1994263–265-69]

#### Lady Diana Spencer

Further information: [Wedding of Prince Charles and Lady Diana Spencer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wedding_of_Prince_Charles_and_Lady_Diana_Spencer) and [Descendants of Elizabeth II § Children of Charles and Diana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Descendants_of_Elizabeth_II#Children_of_Charles_and_Diana)

Charles first met [Lady Diana Spencer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_Diana_Spencer) in 1977, while he was visiting her home, [Althorp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Althorp). He was then the companion of her elder sister Sarah and did not consider Diana romantically until mid-1980. While Charles and Diana were sitting together on a bale of hay at a friend's barbecue in July, she mentioned that he had looked forlorn and in need of care at the [funeral of Lord Mountbatten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funeral_of_Lord_Mountbatten). Soon, according to Dimbleby, "without any apparent surge in feeling, he began to think seriously of her as a potential bride" and she accompanied him on visits to [Balmoral Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balmoral_Castle) and [Sandringham House](977878.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDimbleby1994279-70]

The smiling couple in formal attire. The bride wears a gown with a long train and holds a large bouquet, standing beside the groom in a military uniform adorned with medals.

Charles and Diana on their wedding day, July 1981

Charles's cousin [Norton Knatchbull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norton_Knatchbull,_8th_Baron_Brabourne) and his wife told Charles that Diana appeared awestruck by his position and that he did not seem to be in love with her.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDimbleby1994280–282-71] Meanwhile, the couple's continuing courtship attracted intense attention from the press and paparazzi. When Charles's father told him that the media speculation would injure Diana's reputation if Charles did not come to a decision about marrying her soon, and realising that she was a suitable royal bride (according to Mountbatten's criteria), Charles construed his father's advice as a warning to proceed without further delay.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDimbleby1994281–283-72] He proposed to Diana in February 1981, with their engagement becoming official on 24 February; the wedding took place at [St Paul's Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Paul's_Cathedral) on 29 July. Upon his marriage, Charles reduced his voluntary tax contribution from the profits of the Duchy of Cornwall from 50 per cent to 25 per cent.[^cite_note-73] The couple lived at [Kensington Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kensington_Palace) and [Highgrove House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highgrove_House), near [Tetbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tetbury), and had two children: [William](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William,_Prince_of_Wales), in 1982, and [Harry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Harry,_Duke_of_Sussex), in 1984.[^cite_note-time_1988-23] As of 2025, Charles has an estranged relationship with his son Harry, who relinquished his royal family obligations and moved to the United States in 2020.[^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75]

Within five years, the marriage was in trouble due to the couple's incompatibility and near 13-year age difference.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrown2007720-76][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESmith2000561-77] In 1986 Charles had fully resumed his affair with former girlfriend, Camilla Parker Bowles.[^cite_note-RadioTimes01-78] In a video tape recorded by [Peter Settelen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Settelen) in 1992, Diana admitted that, from 1985 to 1986, she had been "deeply in love with someone who worked in this environment."[^cite_note-79][^cite_note-Telegraph_12Dec2004-80] It was assumed that she was referring to [Barry Mannakee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Mannakee),[^cite_note-81] who had been transferred to the Diplomatic Protection Squad in 1986, after his managers determined his relationship with Diana had been inappropriate.[^cite_note-Telegraph_12Dec2004-80][^cite_note-82] Diana later commenced a relationship with Major [James Hewitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Hewitt), the family's former riding instructor.[^cite_note-83]

Charles and Diana's evident discomfort in each other's company led to them being dubbed "[The Glums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Take_It_from_Here#The_Glums)" by the press.[^cite_note-Quest-84] Diana exposed Charles's affair with Parker Bowles in a book by Andrew Morton, *[Diana: Her True Story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diana:_Her_True_Story_\(book\))*. Audio tapes of [her own extramarital flirtations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Squidgygate) also surfaced,[^cite_note-Quest-84] as did persistent suggestions that Hewitt is Prince Harry's father, based on a physical similarity between Hewitt and Harry. However, Harry had already been born by the time Diana's affair with Hewitt began.[^cite_note-85]

The Waleses and Quayles converse while standing.

Charles and Diana with [Dan Quayle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Quayle) and Marilyn Quayle, November 1990

In December 1992, the [British prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_prime_minister), [John Major](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Major), announced the couple's legal separation in the [House of Commons](13828.md). Early the following year, the British press published transcripts of a passionate, bugged telephone conversation between Charles and Parker Bowles that had taken place in 1989, which was dubbed "[Tampongate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tampongate)" and "Camillagate".[^cite_note-86] Charles subsequently sought public understanding in a television film with Dimbleby, *[Charles: The Private Man, the Public Role](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles:_The_Private_Man,_the_Public_Role)*, broadcast in June 1994. In an interview in the film, Charles confirmed his own extramarital affair with Parker Bowles, saying that he had rekindled their association in 1986, only after his marriage to Diana had "irretrievably broken down".[^cite_note-87][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDimbleby1994395-88] This was followed by Diana's own admission of marital troubles in [an interview](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An_Interview_with_HRH_The_Princess_of_Wales) with journalist [Martin Bashir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Bashir) on the [BBC](19344654.md) current affairs programme *[Panorama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panorama_\(British_TV_programme\))*, broadcast in November 1995.[^cite_note-89] Referring to Charles's relationship with Parker Bowles, she said, "well, there were three of us in this marriage. So, it was a bit crowded." She also expressed doubt about her husband's suitability for kingship.[^cite_note-90] Charles and Diana divorced on 28 August 1996,[^cite_note-91] after being advised by the Queen in December 1995 to end the marriage.[^cite_note-92] The couple shared custody of their children.[^cite_note-93]

Diana [died following a car crash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Diana,_Princess_of_Wales) in Paris on 31 August 1997. Charles flew to Paris with Diana's sisters to accompany her body back to Britain.[^cite_note-94] In 2003, Diana's butler [Paul Burrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Burrell) published a note that he alleged had been written by Diana in 1995, in which there were allegations that Charles was "planning 'an accident' in \[Diana's] car, brake failure and serious head injury", so that he could remarry.[^cite_note-95] She had allegedly expressed similar concerns in October 1995 to [Lord Mishcon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lord_Mishcon), her solicitor, that "reliable sources" had told her "that she and Camilla would be put aside" for Charles to marry [Tiggy Legge-Bourke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiggy_Legge-Bourke).[^cite_note-96] When questioned by the [Metropolitan Police](192450.md) inquiry team as a part of [Operation Paget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Paget), Charles told the authorities that he did not know about his former wife's note from 1995 and could not understand why she had those feelings.[^cite_note-97] The allegations were later revealed to have been among the smears spread by Bashir to secure the interview with Diana for the BBC.[^cite_note-98]

#### Camilla Parker Bowles

Main article: [Wedding of Prince Charles and Camilla Parker Bowles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wedding_of_Prince_Charles_and_Camilla_Parker_Bowles)

Because Charles and Parker Bowles were romantically involved periodically, both before and during their respective first marriages, their relationship received criticism from the public and the media.[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100] Following both of their divorces, Charles declared his relationship with Parker Bowles was "non-negotiable" and appointed [Mark Bolland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Bolland) to enhance Parker Bowles's public profile.[^cite_note-101][^cite_note-102][^cite_note-103]

Charles and two men in suits and uniforms with Camilla in a blue dress stand as they are engaged in conversation.

Charles with Camilla during a visit to the US, November 2005

In 1999 Charles and Parker Bowles made their first public appearance as a couple at the [Ritz London Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ritz_London_Hotel) and the following year, she met Queen Elizabeth II at the 60th birthday party of former [King Constantine II of Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Constantine_II_of_Greece), which was seen as an apparent seal of approval by the Queen on the relationship.[^cite_note-104] In 2003, Parker Bowles moved into Charles's official residence, [Clarence House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarence_House)[^cite_note-105][^cite_note-clarence-106] and she accompanied Charles on almost all of his official events, including the annual [Highland Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highland_Games) in Scotland.[^cite_note-107] Former Archbishop of Canterbury, [George Carey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Carey), told *The Times* that Charles and Parker Bowles should marry, "He is heir to the throne and he loves her. The natural thing is that they should get married."[^cite_note-108] Their engagement was announced on 10 February 2005.[^cite_note-109]  The Queen's consent to the marriage – as required by the [Royal Marriages Act 1772](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Marriages_Act_1772) – was recorded in a [Privy Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Her_Majesty's_Most_Honourable_Privy_Council) meeting on 2 March.[^cite_note-110] In Canada, the [Department of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Justice_\(Canada\)) determined the consent of the [Queen's Privy Council for Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen's_Privy_Council_for_Canada) was not required, as the union would not produce any heirs to the [Canadian throne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_throne).[^cite_note-111]

Charles was the only member of the royal family to have a [civil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_marriage), rather than a church, wedding in England. British government documents from the 1950s and 1960s, published by the BBC, stated that such a marriage was illegal; these claims were dismissed by Charles's spokesman[^cite_note-112] and explained by the sitting government to have been repealed by the Registration Service Act 1953.[^cite_note-113]

The union was scheduled to take place in a civil ceremony at [Windsor Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windsor_Castle), with a subsequent religious blessing at the castle's [St George's Chapel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_George's_Chapel). The wedding venue was changed to [Windsor Guildhall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windsor_Guildhall) after it was realised a civil marriage at Windsor Castle would oblige the venue to be available to anyone who wished to be married there. Four days before the event, it was postponed from the originally scheduled date of 8 April until the following day in order to allow Charles and some of the invited dignitaries to attend the [funeral of Pope John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funeral_of_Pope_John_Paul_II).[^cite_note-114]

Charles's parents did not attend the marriage ceremony; the Queen's reluctance to attend possibly arose from her position as [Supreme Governor of the Church of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Governor_of_the_Church_of_England).[^cite_note-115] However, his parents did attend the [service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_service) of [blessing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blessing) and held a reception for the newlyweds at Windsor Castle.[^cite_note-116] The blessing by Archbishop of Canterbury [Rowan Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rowan_Williams) was televised.[^cite_note-117]

Charles wearing a suit converses with a woman in a dress. Camilla stands next to Charles in the background standing opposite a "Travel Foundation" vertical banner.

Charles and Camilla during a trip to Trinidad and Tobago, March 2008

Since their marriage in 2005, Charles has paid tribute to Camilla repeatedly in public letters, speeches and interviews, thanking her for "steadfast support" and referring to her as "my darling wife".[^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120][^cite_note-121]

### Official duties

See also: [List of official overseas trips made by Charles III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_official_overseas_trips_made_by_Charles_III)

In 1965, Charles undertook his first public engagement by attending a student garden party at the [Palace of Holyroodhouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Holyroodhouse).[^cite_note-coronation-facts-122] During his time as Prince of Wales, he undertook official duties on behalf of the Queen,[^cite_note-nytimes-charlesgothrone-sept82022-123] completing 10,934 engagements between 2002 and 2022.[^cite_note-124] He officiated at [investitures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investiture) and attended the funerals of foreign dignitaries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrandreth2007325-125]

Charles in formal military attire stands at a podium outside, holding a document as he addresses an audience out of frame.

Charles reads Queen Elizabeth II's message during Fijian independence celebration, October 1970

Charles made regular tours of Wales, fulfilling a week of engagements each summer, and attending important national occasions, such as opening the [Senedd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senedd_building).[^cite_note-126] The six trustees of the [Royal Collection Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Collection_Trust) met three times a year under his chairmanship.[^cite_note-127] Charles also represented his mother at the independence celebrations in Fiji in 1970,[^cite_note-128] The Bahamas in 1973,[^cite_note-129] Papua New Guinea in 1975,[^cite_note-130] Zimbabwe in 1980[^cite_note-131] and Brunei in 1984.[^cite_note-132] In 1983, a man named [Christopher John Lewis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_John_Lewis), who had fired a shot with a [.22 rifle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/.22_rifle) at the Queen in 1981, attempted to escape from a psychiatric hospital in order to assassinate Charles, who was visiting New Zealand with Diana and William.[^cite_note-133] While Charles was visiting Australia on [Australia Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_Day) in January 1994, [David Kang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Kang) fired two shots at him from a [starting pistol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starting_pistol) in protest against the treatment of several hundred Cambodian asylum-seekers held in detention camps.[^cite_note-134] In 1995 Charles became the first member of the royal family to visit the Republic of Ireland in an official capacity.[^cite_note-135] In 1997 he represented the Queen at the [Hong Kong handover ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_handover_ceremony).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrendon2007660-136][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrown1998594-137]

At the funeral of Pope John Paul II in 2005, Charles caused controversy when he shook hands with Zimbabwean president [Robert Mugabe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Mugabe), who had been seated next to him. Charles's office subsequently released a statement saying that he could not avoid shaking Mugabe's hand and that he "finds the current Zimbabwean regime abhorrent".[^cite_note-138]

Charles in a suit speaking at a podium during the 2010 Commonwealth Games. Attendees sit around him.

Charles reads a message on behalf of Elizabeth II during the [2010 Commonwealth Games opening ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Commonwealth_Games_opening_ceremony)

Charles represented the Queen at [the opening ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Commonwealth_Games_opening_ceremony) of the [2010 Commonwealth Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Commonwealth_Games) in Delhi, India.[^cite_note-139] In November 2010 he and Camilla were indirectly involved in [student protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_Kingdom_student_protests) when their car was attacked by protesters.[^cite_note-140] In November 2013 he represented the Queen for the first time at a [Commonwealth Heads of Government Meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Heads_of_Government_Meeting), in [Colombo](56636.md), Sri Lanka.[^cite_note-141]

Charles and Camilla made their first joint trip to the Republic of Ireland in May 2015. The British Embassy called the trip an important step in "promoting peace and reconciliation".[^cite_note-142] During the trip, he shook hands in [Galway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galway) with [Gerry Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerry_Adams), leader of [Sinn Féin](28175.md) and widely believed to be the leader of the [IRA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Republican_Army), the militant group that had assassinated Lord Mountbatten in 1979. The event was described by the media as a "historic handshake" and a "significant moment for [Anglo-Irish relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Irish_relations)".[^cite_note-143]

Charles in a blue suit smiles while interacting with a woman in a green dress at a public event. People surround them, capturing the moment with phones.

Charles during the Commonwealth Heads of Government Meeting Big Lunch, April 2018

Commonwealth heads of government decided at their [2018 meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Commonwealth_Heads_of_Government_Meeting) that Charles would be the next Head of the Commonwealth after the Queen.[^cite_note-ny-times-charlesiiichosen-144] The head is chosen and therefore not hereditary.[^cite_note-145] In March 2019, at the request of the British government, Charles and Camilla went on an official tour of Cuba, making them the first British royals to visit the country. The tour was seen as an effort to form a closer relationship between Cuba and the United Kingdom.[^cite_note-146]

Charles contracted [COVID-19](63030231.md) during the [pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_the_United_Kingdom) in March 2020.[^cite_note-147][^cite_note-BBC-virus-148] Several newspapers were critical that Charles and Camilla were tested promptly at a time when many [National Health Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Health_Service) doctors, nurses and patients had been unable to be tested expeditiously.[^cite_note-149] He tested positive for COVID-19 for a second time in February 2022.[^cite_note-150] He and Camilla, who also tested positive, had received doses of a [COVID-19 vaccine](63319438.md) in February 2021.[^cite_note-151]

Charles seated on a throne during the 2022 state opening of the British Parliament. Next to him is the Imperial State Crown.

Delivering the [Queen's Speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speech_from_the_throne) to the British Parliament on behalf of his mother, May 2022

Charles attended the November 2021 ceremonies to mark [Barbados's transition into a parliamentary republic](14456864.md#2021_Constitutional_amendment), abolishing the position of [monarch of Barbados](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarch_of_Barbados).[^cite_note-152] He was invited by Prime Minister [Mia Mottley](15337866.md) as the future Head of the Commonwealth;[^cite_note-153] it was the first time that a member of the royal family attended the transition of a realm to a republic.[^cite_note-154] In May of the following year, Charles attended the [State Opening of the British Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_State_Opening_of_Parliament), delivering the Queen's Speech on behalf of his mother, as a [counsellor of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counsellor_of_state).[^cite_note-155]

## Reign

Charles acceded to the British throne on [his mother's death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_state_funeral_of_Elizabeth_II) on 8 September 2022. He was the longest-serving British heir apparent, having surpassed Edward VII's record of 59 years on 20 April 2011.[^cite_note-apparent-156] Charles was the oldest person to succeed to the British throne, at the age of 73, surpassing the previous record holder, [William IV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_IV), who was 64 when he became king in 1830.[^cite_note-157]

Presiding Officer of the Scottish Parliament Alison Johnstone is seated next to the King.

Addressing the [Scottish Parliament](61188.md) on 13 September 2022

Charles gave his first speech to the nation as monarch at 6 pm on 9 September, in which he paid tribute to his mother and announced the appointment of his elder son, William, as Prince of Wales.[^cite_note-158] The following day, the [Accession Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_Council) publicly [proclaimed Charles as king](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proclamation_of_accession_of_Charles_III), the ceremony being televised for the first time.[^cite_note-159][^cite_note-ny-times-charlesiiichosen-144] Attendees included Queen Camilla, Prince William and the British prime minister, [Liz Truss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liz_Truss), along with her six living predecessors.[^cite_note-160] The proclamation was also read out by local authorities around the United Kingdom. Other realms signed and read their own proclamations, as did Scotland, Wales, Northern Ireland, the [British Overseas Territories](207114.md), the [Crown Dependencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Dependencies), [Canadian provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_provinces) and [Australian states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_states).[^cite_note-161]

In November 2022, the King and Queen hosted the [South African president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_African_president), [Cyril Ramaphosa](1089194.md), during the first official state visit to the United Kingdom of Charles III's reign.[^cite_note-162] The following March, they undertook a state visit to Germany, where Charles became the first British monarch to address the [Bundestag](3768.md).[^cite_note-163] On 6 May 2023, [Charles and Camilla's coronation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_and_Camilla's_coronation) took place at Westminster Abbey,[^cite_note-164] in a ceremony planned for many years under the code name *Operation Golden Orb.*[^cite_note-165][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPepinster2022-166] Prior to Charles's accession, reports suggested that the coronation would be "shorter, smaller, less expensive, and more representative of different faiths and community groups than that of his mother in 1953, reflecting the King's wish to acknowledge the ethnic diversity of modern Britain".[^cite_note-167] Nonetheless, the coronation remained a [Church of England](5955.md) rite, including the [coronation oath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronation_oath), the [anointing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anointing), crowning, investing with the [regalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regalia), and [enthronement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enthronement).[^cite_note-168]

Charles and Camilla wearing their crowns and coronation robes waving from the balcony of Buckingham Palace

Charles and Camilla after their coronation, May 2023

In July 2023, the royal couple attended a [national service of thanksgiving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presentation_of_the_Honours_of_Scotland_to_Charles_III) at [St Giles' Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Giles'_Cathedral), where Charles was presented with the [Honours of Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honours_of_Scotland).[^cite_note-169] That same month, the King asked for the profits from Britain's growing fleet of offshore wind-farms to be used for the "wider public good" rather than as extra funding for the monarchy, and it was announced that the monarchy's share of [Crown Estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Estate) net profits would be reduced to 12 per cent.[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171] In September, Charles became the first British monarch to give a speech from the [French Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Senate) chamber during a [state visit to France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_visit_by_Charles_III_to_France). The following month [he visited Kenya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_visit_by_Charles_III_to_Kenya), where he faced calls to apologise for colonial abuses; at a state banquet he acknowledged "abhorrent and unjustifiable acts of violence" but did not issue a formal apology.[^cite_note-KenyaBanquetSpeech-172]

In May 2024, the British prime minister, [Rishi Sunak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rishi_Sunak), asked the King to call [a general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_Kingdom_general_election); subsequently, royal engagements that could divert attention from the election campaign were postponed.[^cite_note-173] In June 2024, Charles and Camilla travelled to [Normandy](21724.md) to attend the 80th anniversary commemorations of [D-Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D-Day).[^cite_note-174] The same month, the King received [Emperor Naruhito of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emperor_Naruhito_of_Japan) during his state visit to the United Kingdom.[^cite_note-175] In July, the annual Holyrood Week, which is usually spent in Scotland, was shortened so that Charles could return to London and appoint a new prime minister following the general election.[^cite_note-176] After Sunak's [Conservative Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(UK\)) lost the election to the [Labour Party](19279158.md) led by [Sir Keir Starmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_Keir_Starmer), the King appointed Starmer as prime minister.[^cite_note-177]

See caption

With Camilla in front of the [Sydney Harbour Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Harbour_Bridge), October 2024

In October 2024, [the King and Queen toured Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_royal_tour_of_Australia) and [Samoa](27238.md); Australia was the first Commonwealth realm Charles visited since his accession.[^cite_note-178][^cite_note-179] In Samoa, he attended the [Commonwealth Heads of Government Meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Commonwealth_Heads_of_Government_Meeting) for the first time as Head of the Commonwealth.[^cite_note-180] The tour was significantly scaled back owing to his cancer diagnosis, with a planned visit to New Zealand among the cancelled events. It was reported that Charles temporarily paused his cancer treatment during the tour.[^cite_note-181]

In March 2025, the King received Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) at [Sandringham House](977878.md), just days after the [Trump–Zelenskyy Oval Office meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump%E2%80%93Zelenskyy_Oval_Office_meeting). Zelenskyy later told *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* that Charles played a quiet but influential role in altering US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s attitude toward Ukraine.[^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183]

The King and Queen initially cancelled a planned visit to [Vatican City](32408.md) during [their state visit to Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_visit_by_Charles_III_to_Italy), but on 9 April 2025 – their 20th wedding anniversary – they visited [Pope Francis](1687680.md) at [Domus Sanctae Marthae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domus_Sanctae_Marthae) as he was recovering from pneumonia.[^cite_note-184][^cite_note-185] Francis died 12 days later.[^cite_note-186]

Mark Carney leans toward Charles, who is seated and wearing a suit adorned with military medals. A Canadian flag is visible in the background.

Charles seated during the [Canadian Speech from the Throne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Speech_from_the_Throne) in May 2025

The King, accompanied by the Queen, made [his first visit to Canada as monarch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_royal_visit_to_Canada) in May 2025 at the invitation of Prime Minister [Mark Carney](13571049.md), a trip that took place during a period of tension with the United States after President Trump made comments questioning Canada's sovereignty.[^cite_note-187] During the visit, [he opened](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Speech_from_the_Throne) the [45th Canadian Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/45th_Canadian_Parliament) and delivered the Speech from the Throne, the first time a Canadian monarch had done so in person since 1977.[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189]

In June 2025, the King approved the decommissioning of the [British Royal Train](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Royal_Train) ahead of its maintenance contract ending in 2027. Described by the [Keeper of the Privy Purse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keeper_of_the_Privy_Purse) as part of a commitment to "fiscal discipline", the decision marked the end of 180 years of the royal family's use of a dedicated royal train.[^cite_note-190][^cite_note-191]

In October 2025, during a [state visit to the Holy See](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_visit_by_Charles_III_to_the_Holy_See), Charles became the first British monarch to pray alongside a pope since the [Reformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reformation), joining [Pope Leo XIV](54520372.md) for a church service in the [Sistine Chapel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sistine_Chapel) of the [Apostolic Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apostolic_Palace) in Vatican City.[^cite_note-192] Later that month, Charles undertook his first official engagement in support of the [LGBT+ community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT+_community), unveiling "[An Opened Letter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An_Opened_Letter)", the UK's first national memorial honouring LGBT armed forces veterans, at the [National Memorial Arboretum](1430202.md) in [Staffordshire](58733.md).[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194] On 30 October, amid continuing controversy surrounding [his brother Andrew's association](81480934.md) with the American financier and child sex offender [Jeffrey Epstein](6253522.md), the King initiated a formal process to remove his style, titles and honours.[^cite_note-195] Following Andrew's arrest on 19 February 2026 on suspicion of [misconduct in public office](2289190.md), the King expressed his "deepest concern" and stated that "the law must take its course".[^cite_note-196]

In April 2026, during [a state visit to the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_visit_by_Charles_III_to_the_United_States), Charles became the second British monarch to address a [joint meeting of Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Meeting_of_Congress), after his mother [Elizabeth II in 1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_state_visit_by_Elizabeth_II_to_the_United_States).[^cite_note-197] His speech received around a dozen standing ovations.[^cite_note-198] In May, Charles visited [Golders Green](94014.md) after the [previous month's stabbings](83065835.md), meeting victims at a Jewish Care charity centre, the [Chief Rabbi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Rabbi), and members of the community's medical and security response teams.[^cite_note-199] The next month, Buckingham Palace announced that the King would publish his personal tax bills, saying the move was intended to enhance "clarity and accessibility", making him the first UK head of state to do so.[^cite_note-200] In July, the King, acting on the advice of [Deputy Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_prime_minister) [David Lammy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Lammy), granted a [conditional pardon](2176497.md#Conditional_pardons) to [Ruth Ellis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruth_Ellis), the [last woman to be executed in the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_the_United_Kingdom).[^cite_note-201] In September, the King and Queen, along with French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md), viewed the [Bayeux Tapestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayeux_Tapestry) exhibition at the [British Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Museum), marking the first time in more than 900 years that the tapestry had returned to England from France.[^cite_note-202]

## Health

In March 1998, Charles had laser [keyhole surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keyhole_surgery) on his right knee[^cite_note-health-issues-203] and in March 2003, he underwent surgery at [King Edward VII's Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Edward_VII's_Hospital) to treat a [hernia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hernia) injury.[^cite_note-204] In 2008, a non-cancerous growth was removed from his [nasal bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasal_bridge).[^cite_note-health-issues-203]

In January 2024, Charles underwent a "corrective procedure" at [the London Clinic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_London_Clinic) to treat benign [prostate enlargement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prostate_enlargement), which resulted in the postponement of some of his public engagements.[^cite_note-205] In February, Buckingham Palace announced that cancer had been discovered during the treatment, but that it was not [prostate cancer](88078.md). Although his public duties were postponed, it was reported Charles would continue to fulfill his constitutional functions during his [outpatient](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outpatient) treatment.[^cite_note-206] He released a statement espousing his support for cancer charities and that he "remain\[ed] positive" on making a full recovery.[^cite_note-207] In March, Camilla deputised for him in his absence at the [Commonwealth Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Day) service at Westminster Abbey and at the [Royal Maundy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Maundy) at [Worcester Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worcester_Cathedral).[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209] He made his first major public appearance since his cancer diagnosis at the Easter service held at [St George's Chapel, Windsor Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_George's_Chapel,_Windsor_Castle), on 31 March.[^cite_note-210] In April 2024, it was announced that he would resume public-facing duties after making progress in his cancer treatment.[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212] In March 2025, Charles was briefly admitted to hospital after experiencing temporary side effects from his cancer treatment.[^cite_note-Mar25-se-213] He subsequently postponed several planned engagements.[^cite_note-Mar25-se-213] In December, as part of the [Stand Up to Cancer UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stand_Up_to_Cancer_UK) campaign hosted by [Cancer Research UK](615793.md) and [Channel 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channel_4), he announced that his treatment schedule would be reduced the following year.[^cite_note-214] The King's openness about his illness, contrasting with the traditionally guarded approach of earlier monarchs, was described by the media as unprecedented and as bringing him closer to the public.[^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217]

### Diet

As early as 1985, Charles was questioning meat consumption. In the *1985 Royal Special* television programm&#x65;*,* he told the host, [Alastair Burnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alastair_Burnet), that "I actually now don't eat as much meat as I used to. I eat more fish." He also pointed out the societal double standard whereby eating meat is not questioned but eating less meat means "all hell seems to break loose".[^cite_note-218] In 2021, Charles spoke to the BBC about the environment and revealed that, two days per week, he eats no meat nor fish and, one day per week, he eats no dairy products.[^cite_note-219] In 2022, it was reported that he eats a breakfast of fruit salad, seeds, and tea. He does not eat lunch, but takes a break for tea at 5\:00 pm and eats dinner at 8\:30 pm, returning to work until midnight or later.[^cite_note-220] Ahead of [Christmas dinner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_dinner#United_Kingdom) in 2022, Charles confirmed to the animal rights group [People for the Ethical Treatment of Animals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_for_the_Ethical_Treatment_of_Animals) that [foie gras](230149.md) would not be served at any royal residences; he had stopped the use of foie gras at his own properties for more than a decade before becoming king.[^cite_note-221] During a September 2023 state banquet at the [Palace of Versailles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Versailles), it was reported that he did not want foie gras or out-of-season asparagus on the menu. Instead, he was served lobster.[^cite_note-222]

## Charity work

Since founding the [Prince's Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince's_Trust) in 1976, using his £7,500 of [severance pay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Severance_pay) from the Royal Navy,[^cite_note-223] Charles has established 16 more charitable organisations and now serves as president of each.[^cite_note-Charities-224][^cite_note-nytimes-charlesgothrone-sept82022-123] Together they form a loose alliance, [the Prince's Charities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince's_Charities), which describes itself as "the largest multi-cause charitable enterprise in the United Kingdom, raising more than £100 million annually ... \[and is] active across a broad range of areas including education and young people, environmental sustainability, the built environment, responsible business and enterprise, and international".[^cite_note-Charities-224] [King Charles III Charitable Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Charles_III_Charitable_Fund) has donated over £73 million to various causes since 1979.[^cite_note-225] By September 2020 the Prince's Trust had supported over 1,000,000 young people and created 125,000 entrepreneurs.[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227] As Prince of Wales, Charles became [patron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patron) or president of more than 800 other charities and organisations.[^cite_note-coronation-facts-122] He received a Gold [Blue Peter badge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Peter_badge) in 2023 for his environmental work and for his support of young people through the Prince's Trust.[^cite_note-228]

[The Prince's Charities Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince's_Charities_Canada) was established in 2010, in a similar fashion to its namesake in Britain.[^cite_note-229] Charles uses [his tours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_tours_of_Canada) of Canada as a way to help to draw attention to youth, the disabled, the environment, the arts, medicine, the elderly, heritage conservation and education.[^cite_note-charles-canada-visit-230] He has also set up [the Prince's Charities Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince's_Charities_Australia), based in [Melbourne](17306237.md), to provide a coordinating presence for his Australian and international charitable endeavours.[^cite_note-231]



With Camilla visiting the African American Heritage Center in [Louisville, Kentucky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisville,_Kentucky), United States, March 2015

Charles is a long-time supporter of humanitarian projects. In 1980s, he donated his blood to raise awareness for health causes, including [AIDS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIV/AIDS).[^cite_note-232] Charles and his sons took part in ceremonies that marked the 1998 [International Day for the Elimination of Racial Discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Day_for_the_Elimination_of_Racial_Discrimination).[^cite_note-charles-canada-visit-230] Charles expressed strong concerns about the human rights record of the Romanian dictator [Nicolae Ceaușescu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolae_Ceau%C8%99escu).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDimbleby1994250-233] After Ceaușescu was deposed, he supported the FARA Foundation,[^cite_note-PoWsite-16] a charity for Romanian orphans and abandoned children.[^cite_note-234] Charles has also supported humanitarian causes in Israel, including a private donation to the [Peres Center for Peace and Innovation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peres_Center_for_Peace) in 2020.[^cite_note-235]

### Investigations of donations

Main articles: [The King's Foundation § Cash for honours allegations and other donations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_King's_Foundation#Cash_for_honours_allegations_and_other_donations), and [King Charles III Charitable Fund § Qatari donations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Charles_III_Charitable_Fund#Qatari_donations)

Two of Charles's charities, [the Prince's Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince's_Foundation) and [the Prince of Wales's Charitable Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince_of_Wales's_Charitable_Fund) (later renamed the King's Foundation and King Charles III Charitable Fund), came under scrutiny in 2021 and 2022 for accepting donations many in the media deemed inappropriate. In August 2021 it was announced that the Prince's Foundation was launching an investigation into the reports,[^cite_note-236] with Charles's support.[^cite_note-237] The [Charity Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charity_Commission) also launched an investigation into allegations that the donations meant for the Prince's Foundation had been instead sent to the [Mahfouz Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahfouz_Foundation?action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-238] In February 2022, the Metropolitan Police launched an investigation into the cash-for-honours allegations linked to the foundation,[^cite_note-239] passing their evidence to the [Crown Prosecution Service](383064.md) for deliberation in October.[^cite_note-timesnov22-240] In August 2023 the Metropolitan Police announced that they had concluded their investigations and no further action would be taken.[^cite_note-241]

*[The Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times)* reported in June 2022 that, between 2011 and 2015, Charles accepted €3 million in cash from the [Prime Minister of Qatar](3596637.md), [Hamad bin Jassim bin Jaber Al Thani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamad_bin_Jassim_bin_Jaber_Al_Thani).[^cite_note-charlestimes-242][^cite_note-guardiancharles-243] There was no evidence that the payments were illegal or that the money was not intended to go to the charity,[^cite_note-guardiancharles-243] although the Charity Commission stated it would review the information[^cite_note-244] and announced in July 2022 that there would be no further investigation.[^cite_note-245] In the same month, *The Times* reported that the Prince of Wales's Charitable Fund received a donation of £1 million from [Bakr bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakr_bin_Laden) and Shafiq bin Laden – both half-brothers of [Osama bin Laden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osama_bin_Laden) – during a private meeting in 2013.[^cite_note-Times-Bin-Laden-246][^cite_note-Guardian-Bin-Laden-247] The Charity Commission described the decision to accept donations as a "matter for trustees" and added that no investigation was required.[^cite_note-Telegraph-Bin-Laden-248]

## Personal interests

Charles, Hurley, Simon and Kiro, all dressed formally and engaged in conversation in a lavish room with ornate decor.

Charles with governors-general [David Hurley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Hurley) of Australia, [Mary Simon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Simon) of Canada and [Cindy Kiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cindy_Kiro) of New Zealand during a reception at Buckingham Palace, May 2023

From young adulthood, Charles encouraged the understanding of [Indigenous voices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples), saying they held crucial messages about preservation of the land, respecting community and shared values, resolving conflict, and recognising and making good on past iniquities.[^cite_note-249] He dovetailed this view with his efforts against climate change,[^cite_note-250] as well as [reconciliation between Indigenous and non-Indigenous peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truth_and_Reconciliation_Commission_of_Canada) and his charitable work in Canada.[^cite_note-251][^cite_note-252] At the [2022 Commonwealth Heads of Government Meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Commonwealth_Heads_of_Government_Meeting) Charles, who was representing his mother, raised that reconciliation process as an example for dealing with the history of [slavery in the British Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_Britain#Enslaved_Africans),[^cite_note-253] for which he expressed his sorrow.[^cite_note-254]

Letters sent by Charles to British government ministers in 2004 and 2005 expressing his concerns over various policy issues – known as the [black spider memos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_spider_memos) – presented potential embarrassment following a challenge by *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* to release the letters under the [Freedom of Information Act 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_Information_Act_2000). In March 2015 the [Supreme Court of the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_the_United_Kingdom) decided that Charles's letters must be released.[^cite_note-Guardian20150326-255] The [Cabinet Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_Office) published the letters in May that year.[^cite_note-256] The reaction was largely supportive of Charles, with little criticism of him;[^cite_note-post-257] the press variously described the memos as "underwhelming"[^cite_note-258] and "harmless",[^cite_note-259] and concluded that their release had "backfired on those who seek to belittle him".[^cite_note-260] It was revealed in the same year that Charles had access to confidential Cabinet papers.[^cite_note-261]

In October 2020, a letter sent by Charles to the Governor-General of Australia, [Sir John Kerr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sir_John_Kerr), after [Kerr's dismissal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1975_Australian_constitutional_crisis) of Prime Minister [Gough Whitlam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gough_Whitlam) in 1975, was released as part of the collection of [palace letters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_letters) regarding the Australian constitutional crisis.[^cite_note-Guardian-Oct2020-262] In the letter, Charles was supportive of Kerr's decision, writing that what Kerr "did last year was right and the courageous thing to do".[^cite_note-Guardian-Oct2020-262]

Charles standing next to Johnson with the flag of the Commonwealth of Nations behind them

With [Boris Johnson](19065069.md) at the [2022 Commonwealth Heads of Government Meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Commonwealth_Heads_of_Government_Meeting) in [Kigali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kigali), Rwanda

*The Times* reported in June 2022 that Charles had privately described the British government's [Rwanda asylum plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rwanda_asylum_plan) as "appalling" and he feared that it would overshadow the Commonwealth Heads of Government Meeting in Rwanda that same month.[^cite_note-263] It was later claimed that Cabinet ministers had warned Charles to avoid making political comments, as they feared a constitutional crisis could arise if he continued to make such statements once he became King.[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265]

### Built environment

Charles has openly expressed his views on architecture and [urban planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_planning); he fostered the advancement of [New Classical architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Classical_architecture) and asserted that he "care\[s] deeply about issues such as the environment, architecture, inner-city renewal, and the quality of life."[^cite_note-planetizen-266] In a speech given for the 150th anniversary of the [Royal Institute of British Architects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Institute_of_British_Architects) in May 1984, he described a proposed extension to the [National Gallery in London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Gallery_in_London) as a "monstrous carbuncle on the face of a much-loved friend" and deplored the "glass stumps and concrete towers" of modern architecture.[^cite_note-RIBA_speech-267] Charles called for local community involvement in architectural choices and asked, "why has everything got to be vertical, straight, unbending, only at right angles – and functional?"[^cite_note-RIBA_speech-267] Charles has "a deep understanding of [Islamic art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_art) and [architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_architecture)", according to the Oxford professor Mohammed Talib,[^cite_note-268] and has been involved in the construction of a building and garden at the [Oxford Centre for Islamic Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxford_Centre_for_Islamic_Studies) that combine Islamic and Oxford architectural styles.[^cite_note-269]

Charles, wearing a suit, stands with a group of eight people casually dressed, smiling on a grassy field. Trees are visible in the background.

Charles meets urban planning students from [Simon Fraser University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Fraser_University) attending the [INTBAU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/INTBAU) summer program, an architectural nonprofit supported by [The Prince's Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince's_Trust), July 2010

In Charles's 1989 book *[A Vision of Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Vision_of_Britain:_A_Personal_View_of_Architecture)*, and in speeches and essays, he has been critical of [modern architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_architecture), arguing that traditional designs and methods should guide contemporary ones.[^cite_note-270] He has continued to campaign for traditional urbanism, human scale, restoration of historic buildings, and sustainable design[^cite_note-271] despite criticism in the press.[^cite_note-272] Two of his charities – [the Prince's Regeneration Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince's_Regeneration_Trust) and [the Prince's Foundation for Building Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince's_Foundation_for_Building_Community), which were later merged into one charity – promote his views. The village of [Poundbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poundbury) was built on land owned by the [Duchy of Cornwall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duchy_of_Cornwall) to a master plan by [Léon Krier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%A9on_Krier), under the guidance of Charles and in line with his philosophy.[^cite_note-planetizen-266] In 2011, Charles saved the historic [Middleport Pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middleport_Pottery) from permanent closure by leading a £9 million rescue and regeneration project through The Prince's Regeneration Trust.[^cite_note-273] In 2013 developments for the suburb of [Nansledan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nansledan) began on the estate of the Duchy of Cornwall with Charles's endorsement.[^cite_note-274] Charles helped purchase [Dumfries House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dumfries_House) and its complete collection of 18th century furnishings in 2007, taking a £20 million loan from his charitable trust to contribute towards the £45 million cost.[^cite_note-275] The house and gardens remain property of the Prince's Foundation and serve as a museum and community and skills training centre.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277] This led to the development of [Knockroon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knockroon), called the "Scottish Poundbury".[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279] In 2025, it was reported that the design of a new generation of British towns would incorporate Charles's architectural ideas.[^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281]

After lamenting in 1996 the unbridled destruction of many of Canada's historic urban cores, Charles offered his assistance to the [Department of Canadian Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Canadian_Heritage) in creating a trust modelled on Britain's [National Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Trust), a plan that was implemented with the passage of the [federal budget in 2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Canadian_federal_budget).[^cite_note-282] In 1999 Charles agreed to the use of his title for the [Prince of Wales Prize for Municipal Heritage Leadership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Wales_Prize_for_Municipal_Heritage_Leadership), awarded by the [National Trust for Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Trust_for_Canada) to municipal governments that have committed to the conservation of historic places.[^cite_note-283]

Charles warmly shake hands on stage with Scully, with a red curtain backdrop. A gold table in front holds a glass award and a floral arrangement.

Charles accepts the 2005 [Vincent Scully Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_Scully_Prize) from [Vincent Scully](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_Scully)

Whilst visiting the US and surveying the damage caused by [Hurricane Katrina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Katrina), Charles received the [National Building Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Building_Museum)'s [Vincent Scully Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_Scully_Prize) in 2005 for his efforts in regard to architecture; he donated US\$25,000 of the prize money towards restoring storm-damaged communities.[^cite_note-284] For his work as patron of New Classical architecture, Charles was awarded the 2012 [Driehaus Architecture Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Driehaus_Architecture_Prize) from the [University of Notre Dame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Notre_Dame).[^cite_note-285] The [Worshipful Company of Carpenters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worshipful_Company_of_Carpenters) installed Charles as an Honorary Liveryman "in recognition of his interest in London's architecture".[^cite_note-286]

Charles has occasionally intervened in projects that employ architectural styles such as [modernism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modernism) and [functionalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Functionalism_\(architecture\)).[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-ArchCon14-288] In 2009 he wrote to the [Qatari royal family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatari_royal_family) – the financier of the redevelopment of the [Chelsea Barracks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chelsea_Barracks) site – labelling [Lord Rogers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lord_Rogers)'s design for the site "unsuitable". Rogers claimed that Charles had also intervened to block his designs for the [Royal Opera House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Opera_House) and [Paternoster Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paternoster_Square).[^cite_note-ArchCon2-289] [CPC Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CPC_Group), the project developer, took a case against [Qatari Diar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatari_Diar) to the [High Court](26569402.md).[^cite_note-BBC-CPC_group-290] After the suit was settled, the CPC Group apologised to Charles "for any offence caused ... during the course of the proceedings".[^cite_note-BBC-CPC_group-290]

### Natural environment

Since the 1970s, Charles has promoted environmental awareness.[^cite_note-291] At the age of 21 he delivered his first speech on [environmental issues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_the_United_Kingdom) in his capacity as the chairman of the Welsh Countryside Committee.[^cite_note-292] Charles was named one of [Time Magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Magazine)'s 100 most influential climate leaders of 2025.[^cite_note-293] An avid gardener, Charles has also emphasised the importance of [talking to plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talking_to_plants), stating that "I happily talk to the plants and trees, and listen to them. I think it's absolutely crucial".[^cite_note-294] His interest in gardening began in 1980 when he took over the Highgrove estate.[^cite_note-Guardian-garden-295] His "healing garden", based on sacred geometry and ancient religious symbolism, went on display at the [Chelsea Flower Show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chelsea_Flower_Show) in 2002.[^cite_note-Guardian-garden-295]



With Camilla visiting [Hackney City Farm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hackney_City_Farm) in East London, May 2009

Upon moving into Highgrove House, Charles developed an interest in [organic farming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organic_farming), which culminated in the 1990 launch of his own organic brand, [Duchy Originals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duchy_Originals),[^cite_note-duchyoriginals-296] which sells more than 200 different sustainably produced products; the profits (more than £6 million by 2010) are donated to the Prince's Charities.[^cite_note-duchyoriginals-296][^cite_note-297] Charles became involved with farming and various industries within it, regularly meeting with farmers to discuss their trade. A prominent critic of the practice,[^cite_note-298] Charles has also spoken against the use of [genetically modified crops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genetically_modified_crops), and in a letter to [Tony Blair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Blair) in 1998 he criticised the development of [genetically modified foods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genetically_modified_food).[^cite_note-299]

The Sustainable Markets Initiative – a project that encourages putting [sustainability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainability) at the centre of all activities – was launched by Charles at the [World Economic Forum](172185.md)'s annual meeting in [Davos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davos) in January 2020.[^cite_note-300] In May of the same year, the initiative and the World Economic Forum initiated the [Great Reset](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Reset) project, a five-point plan concerned with enhancing sustainable economic growth following the global recession caused by the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md).[^cite_note-301]

The holy [chrism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chrism) oil used at Charles's coronation was vegan, made from oils of olive, sesame, rose, jasmine, cinnamon, neroli and benzoin, along with amber and orange blossom. His mother's chrism oil contained animal-based oils.[^cite_note-302]
