Charles delivers a speech at a podium with a backdrop with the logo of COP 26 and United Nations Climate Change

Addressing the opening of [COP26](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COP26), November 2021

Charles delivered a speech at the [2021 G20 Rome summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_G20_Rome_summit), describing [COP26](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COP26) as "the last chance saloon" for preventing climate change and asking for actions that would lead to a green-led, sustainable economy.[^cite_note-303] In his speech at the opening ceremony for COP26, he repeated his sentiments from the previous year, stating that "a vast military-style campaign" was needed "to marshal the strength of the global private sector" for tackling climate change.[^cite_note-304] In 2022, the media alleged that Liz Truss had advised Charles against attending [COP27](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COP27), to which advice he agreed.[^cite_note-305] Charles delivered the opening speech at [COP28](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COP28), saying among others he prayed "with all my heart that COP28 will be a critical turning point towards genuine transformational action."[^cite_note-306] He received a Green Blue Peter badge on [Earth Day 2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earth_Day_2026) in recognition of his long‑standing support for environmental protection and sustainability.[^cite_note-307]

Charles, who is patron of the [Cambridge Institute for Sustainability Leadership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambridge_Institute_for_Sustainability_Leadership), introduced the Climate Action Scholarships for students from small island nations in partnership with the [University of Cambridge](25978572.md), the [University of Toronto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Toronto), the [University of Melbourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Melbourne), [McMaster University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McMaster_University) and the [University of Montreal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Montreal) in March 2022.[^cite_note-308] In 2010, he funded the Prince's Countryside Fund (renamed [the Royal Countryside Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Royal_Countryside_Fund) in 2023), a charity that aims for a "confident, robust and sustainable agricultural and rural community".[^cite_note-Prince-309]

### Alternative medicine

Charles has controversially championed [alternative medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_medicine), including [homeopathy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeopathy).[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-\:1-311] He first publicly expressed his interest in the topic in December 1982, in an address to the [British Medical Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Medical_Association).[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] This speech was seen as "combative" and "critical" of modern medicine and was met with anger by some medical professionals.[^cite_note-\:1-311] Similarly, [the Prince's Foundation for Integrated Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prince's_Foundation_for_Integrated_Health) (FIH) attracted opposition from the scientific and medical community over its campaign encouraging [general practitioners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_practitioner) to offer [herbal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbal_medicine) and other alternative treatments to NHS patients.[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-guardian-coffee-cancer-315]

In April 2008, *[The Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times)* published a letter from [Edzard Ernst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edzard_Ernst), professor of [complementary medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complementary_medicine) at the [University of Exeter](33719893.md), which asked the FIH to recall two guides promoting alternative medicine. That year, Ernst published a book with [Simon Singh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Singh) called *[Trick or Treatment: Alternative Medicine on Trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trick_or_Treatment:_Alternative_Medicine_on_Trial)* and mockingly dedicated to "HRH the Prince of Wales". The last chapter is highly critical of Charles's advocacy of complementary and alternative treatments.[^cite_note-316]

Charles's Duchy Originals produced a variety of complementary medicinal products, including a "Detox Tincture" that Ernst denounced as "financially exploiting the vulnerable" and "outright [quackery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quackery)".[^cite_note-TelegraphAltMed-317] Charles personally wrote at least seven letters[^cite_note-318] to the [Medicines and Healthcare products Regulatory Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicines_and_Healthcare_products_Regulatory_Agency) shortly before it relaxed the rules governing labelling of such herbal products, a move that was widely condemned by scientists and medical bodies.[^cite_note-319] It was reported in October 2009 that Charles had lobbied the health secretary, [Andy Burnham](414818.md), regarding greater provision of alternative treatments in the NHS.[^cite_note-TelegraphAltMed-317]

Following accounting irregularities, the FIH announced its closure in April 2010.[^cite_note-320][^cite_note-GuardianCollege-321] The FIH was re-branded and re-launched later in 2010 as [the College of Medicine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_College_of_Medicine),[^cite_note-GuardianCollege-321][^cite_note-322] of which Charles became a patron in 2019.[^cite_note-323]

### Sports

Charles and others on horseback during a game of polo

Charles (at front) at the 2005 Chakravarty Cup Match at Ham Polo Club, June 2005

From his youth until 2005, Charles was an avid player of competitive [polo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polo).[^cite_note-324] Charles also frequently took part in [fox hunting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_hunting) until [the sport was banned in the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunting_Act_2004), also in 2005.[^cite_note-325] By the late 1990s opposition to the activity was growing when Charles's participation was viewed as a "political statement" by those who were opposed to it.[^cite_note-326] Charles suffered several polo- and hunting-related injuries throughout the years, including a two-inch scar on his left cheek in 1980, a broken arm in 1990, a torn cartilage in his left knee in 1992, a broken rib in 1998, and a fractured shoulder in 2001.[^cite_note-health-issues-203]

Charles has been a keen salmon angler since youth and supported [Orri Vigfússon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orri_Vigf%C3%BAsson)'s efforts to protect the North Atlantic salmon. He frequently fishes the [River Dee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/River_Dee,_Aberdeenshire) in [Aberdeenshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aberdeenshire), and claims his most special angling memories are from his time spent in [Vopnafjörður](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vopnafj%C3%B6r%C3%B0ur), Iceland.[^cite_note-327] Charles is a supporter of [Burnley F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burnley_F.C.)[^cite_note-328]

Apart from hunting, Charles has also participated in target rifle competitions, representing the House of Lords in the Vizianagram Match (Lords vs. Commons) at [Bisley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bisley_Camp).[^cite_note-329] He became President of the British [National Rifle Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rifle_Association_\(United_Kingdom\)) in 1977.[^cite_note-330]

### Visual, performing, and literary arts

Charles has been involved in performance since his youth, and appeared in [sketches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sketch_comedy) and [revues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revue) while studying at Cambridge.[^cite_note-331]

Charles in a brown coat attending the performance in Stratford-upon-Avon

At a performance of *[Henry V](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_V_\(play\))* at the [Courtyard Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Courtyard_Theatre) in 2007

Charles is president or patron of more than 20 performing arts organisations, including the [Royal College of Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_College_of_Music), [Royal Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Royal_Opera), [English Chamber Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Chamber_Orchestra), [Philharmonia Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philharmonia_Orchestra), [Welsh National Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welsh_National_Opera), [Royal Shakespeare Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Shakespeare_Company) (attending performances in [Stratford-Upon-Avon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stratford-Upon-Avon), supporting fundraising events, and attending the company's annual general meeting),[^cite_note-arts-332] [British Film Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Film_Institute),[^cite_note-333] and [Purcell School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purcell_School). In 2000 he revived the tradition of appointing [an official harpist to the Prince of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_Harpist_to_the_Prince_of_Wales), in order to foster Welsh talent at playing the national instrument of Wales.[^cite_note-334] The role, now referred to as King's Harpist, is currently held by [Mared Pugh Evans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mared_Pugh_Evans).[^cite_note-Pugh-Evans-335]

Charles is a keen [watercolourist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watercolour), having published books on the subject and exhibited and sold a number of his works to raise money for charity; in 2016 it was estimated that he had sold lithographs of his watercolours for a total of £2 million from a shop at his Highgrove House residence. For his 50th birthday 50 of his watercolours were exhibited at [Hampton Court Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hampton_Court_Palace) and, for his 70th birthday, his works were exhibited at the [National Gallery of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Gallery_of_Australia).[^cite_note-CNN-Watercolours-336] Charles founded the [Royal Drawing School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Drawing_School) in 2000, which offers drawing classes to children and the public.[^cite_note-337] In 2001, 20 [lithographs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithographs) of his watercolour paintings illustrating his country estates were exhibited at the [Florence International Biennale of Contemporary Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence_Biennale)[^cite_note-338] and 79 of his paintings were put on display in London in 2022. To mark the 25th anniversary of his investiture as Prince of Wales in 1994, the [Royal Mail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Mail) issued a series of postage stamps that featured his paintings.[^cite_note-CNN-Watercolours-336] Charles is Honorary President of the [Royal Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Academy) of Arts Development Trust[^cite_note-339] and, in 2015, 2022 and 2023, commissioned paintings of 12 D-Day veterans, [seven Holocaust survivors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_Portraits:_Surviving_the_Holocaust), and ten members of the [Windrush generation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windrush_generation), respectively, which went on display at the Queen's Gallery in Buckingham Palace.[^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342]

Charles [is the author of several books](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bibliography_of_Charles_III) and has contributed a [foreword](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreword) or [preface](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preface) to numerous books by others. He has also been featured in a variety of documentary films.[^cite_note-343]

### Religion and philosophy

Shortly after his accession to the throne, Charles publicly described himself as "a committed [Anglican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglican) Christian";[^cite_note-Davies16Sep22-344] at age 16, during Easter 1965, he had been [confirmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confirmed) into the [Anglican Communion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglican_Communion) by Archbishop of Canterbury [Michael Ramsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Ramsey) in St George's Chapel, Windsor Castle.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolden1979141–142-345] The King is the Supreme Governor of the Church of England[^cite_note-346] and a member of the [Church of Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_Scotland); he swore an oath to uphold that church immediately after he was proclaimed King.[^cite_note-347] He attends services at various Anglican churches close to Highgrove[^cite_note-348] and attends the Church of Scotland's [Crathie Kirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crathie_Kirk) with the rest of the royal family when staying at Balmoral Castle.[^cite_note-349]

Charles conversing with Šuvarský

With [Czech Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_Orthodox) priest [Jaroslav Šuvarský](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaroslav_%C5%A0uvarsk%C3%BD?action=edit\&redlink=1) in [Prague](23844.md), Czech Republic, March 2010

[Laurens van der Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurens_van_der_Post) became a friend of Charles in 1977; he was dubbed Charles's "spiritual guru" and was godfather to Prince William.[^cite_note-350] From van der Post, Charles developed a focus on philosophy and an interest in other religions.[^cite_note-351] Charles expressed his philosophical views in his 2010 book, *[Harmony: A New Way of Looking at Our World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harmony:_A_New_Way_of_Looking_at_Our_World)*,[^cite_note-352] which won a Nautilus Book Award.[^cite_note-353] He has also visited [Eastern Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox) monasteries on [Mount Athos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Athos),[^cite_note-354] in Romania,[^cite_note-ref1-355] and in Serbia,[^cite_note-356] and met with Eastern Church leaders in Jerusalem in 2020, during a visit that culminated in an ecumenical service in the [Church of the Nativity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_the_Nativity) in Bethlehem and a walk through the city accompanied by Christian and Muslim dignitaries.[^cite_note-357] Charles also attended the consecration of Britain's first [Syriac Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syriac_Orthodox) cathedral, [St Thomas Cathedral, Acton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Thomas_Cathedral,_Acton).[^cite_note-358] Charles is patron of the [Oxford Centre for Islamic Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxford_Centre_for_Islamic_Studies) at the [University of Oxford](31797.md) and attended the inauguration of the [Markfield Institute of Higher Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markfield_Institute_of_Higher_Education), which is dedicated to Islamic studies in a multicultural context.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360]

In his 1994 documentary with Dimbleby, Charles said that, when king, he wished to be seen as a "defender of faith", rather than the British monarch's traditional title of *[Defender of the Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defender_of_the_Faith)*, "prefer\[ring] to embrace all religious traditions and 'the pattern of the divine, which I think is in all of us.'"[^cite_note-361] This attracted controversy at the time, as well as speculation that the [coronation oath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronation_oath_of_the_British_monarch) might be altered.[^cite_note-362] He stated in 2015 that he would retain the title of *Defender of the Faith*, whilst "ensuring that other people's faiths can also be practised", which he sees as a duty of the Church of England.[^cite_note-363] Charles reaffirmed this theme shortly after his accession and declared that his duties as sovereign included "the duty to protect the diversity of our country, including by protecting the space for faith itself and its practice through the religions, cultures, traditions, and beliefs to which our hearts and minds direct us as individuals."[^cite_note-Davies16Sep22-344] Although the text of the coronation oath was not altered, because the process of passing new legislation through Parliament would be lengthy and liable to raise divisive issues, a [preamble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preamble) spoken by the [Archbishop of Canterbury](2345.md) was added to the text:[^cite_note-364]

> Your Majesty, the Church established by law, whose settlement you will swear to maintain, is committed to the true profession of the Gospel, and, in so doing, will seek to foster an environment in which people of all faiths and beliefs may live freely.[^cite_note-365]

His inclusive, multi-faith approach and his own Christian beliefs were expressed in his first [Christmas message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_message) as king. In line with his multi-faith approach, Charles has served as a patron of the [Council of Christians and Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Christians_and_Jews) and of the [Jewish Museum London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Museum_London).[^cite_note-Jewish-Museum-366][^cite_note-367]

## Media image and public opinion

Further information: [Cultural depictions of Charles III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_depictions_of_Charles_III)

Since his birth, Charles has received close media attention, which increased as he matured. It has been an ambivalent relationship, largely impacted by his marriages to Diana and Camilla and their aftermath, but also centred on his future conduct as king.[^cite_note-368]

Charles and another man dressed in a suit discuss space exploration beside models of a spacecraft and Jupiter as a crowd watches behind them.

Charles visiting [Jet Propulsion Laboratory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jet_Propulsion_Laboratory) in the US, October 1977. A camera crew documenting his visit there is visible in the background.

Described as the "[world's most eligible bachelor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eligible_bachelor)" in the late 1970s,[^cite_note-369] Charles was subsequently overshadowed by Diana.[^cite_note-370] After her death the media regularly breached Charles's privacy and printed exposés. Known for expressing his opinions, when asked during an interview to mark his 70th birthday whether this would continue in the same way once he is King, he responded "No. It won't. I'm not that stupid. I do realise that it is a separate exercise being sovereign. So, of course, you know, I understand entirely how that should operate."[^cite_note-371] In September 2025, *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* analysed all Charles's public speeches over the past 30 years and concluded that he has mellowed over the past three decades.[^cite_note-372]

In 2009, Charles was named the world's best-dressed man by *[Esquire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esquire_\(magazine\))* magazine. Fashion magazine *[British GQ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_GQ)* described Charles as "the true guardian of traditional British menswear" in 2012 and named him one of the most stylish people of 2025.[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] Italian designer [Donatella Versace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donatella_Versace) once said Charles "is an archetype of style".[^cite_note-376] In 2023 the *[New Statesman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Statesman)* named Charles as the fourth-most-powerful right-wing figure of the year, describing him as a "[romantic traditionalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditionalism_\(perennialism\))" and "the very last [reactionary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactionary) in public life" for his support of various traditionalist [think tanks](37101.md) and previous writings.[^cite_note-377] He was also named one of the [100 most influential people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_100) of 2023 by *[Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_\(magazine\))* magazine.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] Charles and Camilla topped *Tatler*'s Social Power Index for 2022 and 2023.[^cite_note-380]

See caption

Charles wearing a grey double breasted suit during an official visit to Brazil, March 2009

A 2018 BMG Research poll found that 46 per cent of Britons wanted Charles to abdicate immediately on his mother's death, in favour of William.[^cite_note-381] However, a 2021 opinion poll reported that 60 percent of the British public had a favourable opinion of him.[^cite_note-382] On his accession to the throne, *[The Statesman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Statesman_\(India\))* reported an opinion poll that put Charles's popularity with the British people at 42 per cent.[^cite_note-383] More recent polling suggested that his popularity increased sharply after he became King.[^cite_note-384] On the first anniversary of his reign, a [YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov) poll found that 59 per cent of Britons said he was doing a good job.[^cite_note-385] *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* wrote that his first year as king had proved "unexpectedly popular".[^cite_note-386] As of July 2026, Charles continued to hold majority support, with an approval rating of 62 per cent, and 68 percent of respondents saying "he is doing a good job", according to YouGov.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-388]

### Reaction to press treatment

In 1994, the German tabloid *[Bild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bild)* published nude photos of Charles that were taken while he was vacationing in [Le Barroux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Barroux); they had reportedly been put up for sale for £30,000.[^cite_note-Independent-Sept94-389] Buckingham Palace reacted by stating that it was "unjustifiable for anybody to suffer this sort of intrusion".[^cite_note-390]

Charles, "so often a target of the press, got his chance to return fire" in 2002, when addressing "scores of editors, publishers, and other media executives" gathered at [St Bride's Fleet Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Bride's_Fleet_Street) to celebrate 300 years of journalism.[^cite_note-391][^cite_note-Woods-392] Defending public servants from "the corrosive drip of constant criticism", he noted that the press had been "awkward, cantankerous, cynical, bloody-minded, at times intrusive, at times inaccurate, and at times deeply unfair and harmful to individuals and to institutions".[^cite_note-Woods-392] However, he concluded, regarding his own relations with the press, "from time to time we are probably both a bit hard on each other, exaggerating the downsides and ignoring the good points in each".[^cite_note-Woods-392]

Charles and Camilla amidst a crowd of people, mostly reporters and photographers

With Camilla (centre left) in front of the media pack in the [French Quarter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Quarter) of [New Orleans](53842.md), United States, as part of [Hurricane Katrina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurricane_Katrina) recovery efforts, November 2005

In 2006, Charles [filed a court case](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Wales_v_Associated_Newspapers_Ltd) against *[The Mail on Sunday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Mail_on_Sunday)*, after excerpts of his personal journals were published, revealing his opinions on matters such as the [transfer of sovereignty over Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transfer_of_sovereignty_over_Hong_Kong) to China in 1997, in which Charles described the Chinese government officials as "appalling old waxworks".[^cite_note-BBCjournals-393][^cite_note-nytimes-charlesgothrone-sept82022-123] Charles and Camilla were named in 2011 as [individuals whose confidential information was reportedly targeted or actually acquired](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_news_media_phone_hacking_scandal_victims) in conjunction with the [news media phone hacking scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News_media_phone_hacking_scandal).[^cite_note-394]

*[The Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Independent)* noted in 2015 that Charles would only speak to broadcasters "on the condition they have signed a 15-page contract, demanding that Clarence House attends both the 'rough cut' and 'fine cut' edits of films and, if it is unhappy with the final product, can 'remove the contribution in its entirety from the programme'".[^cite_note-I.Burrell15-395] This contract stipulated that all questions directed at Charles must be pre-approved and [vetted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vetted) by his representatives.[^cite_note-I.Burrell15-395]

## Residences and finance

Further information: [Finances of the British royal family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finances_of_the_British_royal_family)

&#x20;In 2023, *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* estimated Charles's personal wealth at £1.8 billion.[^cite_note-\:0-396] This estimate includes the assets of the [Duchy of Lancaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duchy_of_Lancaster) worth £653 million (and paying Charles an annual income of £20 million), jewels worth £533 million, real estate worth £330 million, shares and investments worth £142 million, [a stamp collection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Philatelic_Collection) worth at least £100 million, racehorses worth £27 million, artworks worth £24 million and cars worth £6.3 million.[^cite_note-\:0-396] Most of this wealth, which he inherited from his mother, [Elizabeth II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_II), was exempt from inheritance tax.[^cite_note-\:0-396][^cite_note-397]

Photograph of the white building with a Union flag flying over it

[Clarence House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarence_House), Charles's primary London residence since 2003

Clarence House, previously the residence of the Queen Mother, has been Charles's official London residence from 2003, after being renovated at a cost of £6.1 million.[^cite_note-398] He previously shared apartments eight and nine at [Kensington Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kensington_Palace) with Diana before moving to [York House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/York_House,_St_James's_Palace) at [St James's Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_James's_Palace), which remained his principal residence until 2003.[^cite_note-clarence-106] [Highgrove House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highgrove_House) in [Gloucestershire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gloucestershire) is owned by the [Duchy of Cornwall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duchy_of_Cornwall), having been purchased for Charles's use in 1980, and which he rented for £336,000 per annum.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETemple2012-399][^cite_note-400] Since [William](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William,_Prince_of_Wales) became [Duke of Cornwall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duke_of_Cornwall), Charles is expected to pay £700,000 per annum for use of the property.[^cite_note-401] Charles also owns a property near the village of [Viscri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viscri) in [Romania](25445.md).[^cite_note-402][^cite_note-403]

As [Prince of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Wales), Charles's primary source of income was generated from the Duchy of Cornwall, which owns 133,658 acres of land (around 54,090 hectares), including farming, residential, and commercial properties, as well as an investment portfolio. Since 1993, he has paid tax voluntarily under the *Memorandum of Understanding on Royal Taxation*, updated in 2013.[^cite_note-404] [Her Majesty's Revenue and Customs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Her_Majesty's_Revenue_and_Customs) were asked in December 2012 to investigate alleged [tax avoidance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_avoidance) by the Duchy of Cornwall.[^cite_note-405] The Duchy was named in the [Paradise Papers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paradise_Papers), a set of confidential electronic documents relating to [offshore investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Offshore_investment) that were leaked to the German newspaper *[Süddeutsche Zeitung](30873120.md)* in 2017.[^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407] In the 2021–22 financial year, Charles received £23 million from the Duchy of Cornwall and paid a voluntary income tax of £5.9 million.[^cite_note-408]

In June 2026, Charles revealed that he had paid £12.9 million in tax for the 2024–25 financial year, and it was also announced that, once the renovations at [Buckingham Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buckingham_Palace) are completed, he and Camilla will not move into the palace but will instead continue living at Clarence House.[^cite_note-409]

## Titles, styles, honours, and arms

### Titles and styles

Charles has held many titles and honorary military positions throughout the Commonwealth, is sovereign of many orders in his own countries and has received honours and awards from around the world.[^cite_note-412][^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414][^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416] In each of his realms he has a distinct title that follows a similar formula: *King of Saint Lucia and of His other Realms and Territories* in [Saint Lucia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Saint_Lucia), *King of Australia and His other Realms and Territories* in Australia, etc. In the [Isle of Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isle_of_Man), which is a [Crown Dependency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_Dependency) rather than a separate realm, he is known as [Lord of Mann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lord_of_Mann). Charles is also styled *Defender of the Faith*.

There had been speculation throughout Elizabeth II's reign as to what [regnal name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regnal_name) Charles would choose upon his accession; instead of *Charles III*, he could have chosen to reign as *George VII* or used one of his other given names.[^cite_note-417] It was reported that he might use George in honour of his grandfather George VI and to avoid associations with previous controversial kings named Charles.[^cite_note-419][^cite_note-CallMeGeorge-418][^cite_note-420] Charles's office asserted in 2005 that no decision had yet been made.[^cite_note-421] Speculation continued for a few hours following his mother's death,[^cite_note-422] until Prime Minister Liz Truss announced and Clarence House confirmed that Charles had chosen the regnal name *Charles III*.[^cite_note-423][^cite_note-424]

Charles, who left active military service in 1976, was awarded the highest rank in all three armed services in 2012 by his mother: [Admiral of the Fleet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admiral_of_the_Fleet_\(Royal_Navy\)), [Field Marshal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_marshal_\(United_Kingdom\)), and [Marshal of the Royal Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marshal_of_the_Royal_Air_Force).[^cite_note-ward-425]

### Arms

Further information: [Coat of arms of the Prince of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_the_Prince_of_Wales), [Coat of arms of the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_the_United_Kingdom), and [Coat of arms of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Canada)

As Prince of Wales, Charles's coat of arms was based on the arms of the United Kingdom, differenced with a white label and an [inescutcheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inescutcheon) of the Principality of Wales, surmounted by the heir apparent's crown, and with the motto *Ich dien* (German: [\[ɪç ˈdiːn\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Standard_German), "I serve") instead of *[Dieu et mon droit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dieu_et_mon_droit)*.

&#x20;When Charles became king, he inherited the royal coats of arms of the United Kingdom and of Canada.[^cite_note-426] The design of his royal cypher, featuring a depiction of the [Tudor Crown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tudor_Crown) instead of [St Edward's Crown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Edward's_Crown), was revealed on 27 September 2022. The College of Arms envisaged that the Tudor crown will be used in new arms, uniforms and crown badges as they are replaced.[^cite_note-427] On 10 October 2024, designed by artist Timothy Noad, the new [Royal Arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Arms), alongside the new Lesser Arms, used by the UK government were officially unveiled, with both arms reflecting the stylistic changes made after Charles's accession.[^cite_note-428]

### Banners, flags, and standards

See also: [Flags of Charles III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flags_of_Charles_III)

#### As heir apparent

The banners used by Charles as Prince of Wales varied depending upon location. His personal standard for the United Kingdom was the [Royal Standard of the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Standard_of_the_United_Kingdom) differenced as in his arms, with a label of three points *argent* and the escutcheon of the arms of the Principality of Wales in the centre. It was used outside Wales, Scotland, Cornwall and Canada, and throughout the entire United Kingdom when Charles was acting in an official capacity associated with the British Armed Forces.[^cite_note-flags-429]

The personal flag for use in Wales was based upon the [Royal Badge of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Badge_of_Wales).[^cite_note-flags-429] In Scotland the personal banner used between 1974 and 2022 was based upon three ancient Scottish titles: [Duke of Rothesay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duke_of_Rothesay) (heir apparent to the [King of Scots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_Scots)), [High Steward of Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Steward_of_Scotland), and Lord of the Isles. In Cornwall, the banner was the arms of the Duke of Cornwall.[^cite_note-flags-429]

In 2011, the [Canadian Heraldic Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Heraldic_Authority) introduced a [personal heraldic banner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_standards_of_Canada#Other_members_of_the_royal_family) for the [Prince of Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Wales) for Canada, consisting of the shield of the [Royal Coat of Arms of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Coat_of_Arms_of_Canada) defaced with both a blue [roundel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roundel_\(heraldry\)) of the [Prince of Wales's feathers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_of_Wales's_feathers) surrounded by a wreath of gold maple leaves and a white label of three points.[^cite_note-430]

#### As sovereign

Main articles: [Royal Standard of the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Standard_of_the_United_Kingdom), [Royal standards of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_standards_of_Canada), and [King's Flag for Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King's_Flag_for_Australia)

The royal standard of the United Kingdom is used to represent the King in the United Kingdom and on official visits overseas, except in Australia and Canada. It is the royal arms in banner form undifferentiated, having been used by successive British monarchs since 1702. The royal standard of Canada is used by the King in Canada and while acting on behalf of Canada overseas. It is the *[escutcheon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Escutcheon_\(heraldry\))* of the Royal Coat of Arms of Canada in banner form undifferentiated.

[^cite_note-realms-3]: &#x20;The 14 other realms are [Antigua and Barbuda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Antigua_and_Barbuda), [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Australia), [The Bahamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_the_Bahamas), [Belize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Belize), [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Canada), [Grenada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Grenada), [Jamaica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Jamaica), [New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_New_Zealand), [Papua New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Papua_New_Guinea), [Saint Kitts and Nevis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Saint_Kitts_and_Nevis), [Saint Lucia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Saint_Lucia), [Saint Vincent and the Grenadines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Saint_Vincent_and_the_Grenadines), the [Solomon Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Solomon_Islands) and [Tuvalu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy_of_Tuvalu).

[^cite_note-5]: &#x20;As the reigning monarch, Charles does not usually use a family name, but when one is needed, it is [Mountbatten-Windsor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountbatten-Windsor).<sup>[\[2\]](#cite_note-royal_family_name-4)</sup>

[^cite_note-6]: &#x20;As monarch, Charles is [Supreme Governor of the Church of England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Governor_of_the_Church_of_England) and a member of the [Church of Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_Scotland).

[^cite_note-7]: &#x20;The Prince's Trust was renamed to the King's Trust following his accession to the throne.

[^cite_note-13]: &#x20;He was reportedly named "Charles" after his godfather [Haakon VII of Norway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haakon_VII_of_Norway) (born Prince Carl of Denmark), who was called "Uncle Charles" by [Elizabeth II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_II).<sup>[\[6\]](#cite_note-11)</sup><sup>[\[7\]](#cite_note-12)</sup>

[^cite_note-15]: &#x20;Prince Charles's [godparents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Godparent) were: the [King of the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_VI) (his maternal grandfather); the [King of Norway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haakon_VII_of_Norway) (his paternal cousin twice removed and maternal great-great-uncle by marriage, for whom Charles's great-great-uncle the [Earl of Athlone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Cambridge,_1st_Earl_of_Athlone) stood proxy); [Queen Mary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_of_Teck) (his maternal great-grandmother); [Princess Margaret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princess_Margaret) (his maternal aunt); [Prince George of Greece and Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_George_of_Greece_and_Denmark) (his paternal great-uncle, for whom the Duke of Edinburgh stood proxy); the [Dowager Marchioness of Milford Haven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princess_Victoria_of_Hesse_and_by_Rhine) (his paternal great-grandmother); the [Lady Brabourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patricia_Knatchbull,_2nd_Countess_Mountbatten_of_Burma) (his cousin); and the Hon [David Bowes-Lyon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Bowes-Lyon) (his maternal great-uncle).<sup>[\[8\]](#cite_note-14)</sup>

[^cite_note-68]: &#x20;Mountbatten had served as the last British [viceroy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroy) and first [governor-general of India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor-general_of_India).

[^cite_note-391]: &#x20;London's first daily newspaper, the *[Daily Courant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_Courant)*, was published in 1702.

[^cite_note-419]: &#x20;Namely, the [Stuart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Stuart) kings [Charles I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_I_of_England), who [was beheaded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Execution_of_Charles_I), and [Charles II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_II_of_England), who was known for his promiscuous lifestyle. [Charles Edward Stuart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Edward_Stuart), once a Stuart pretender to the English and Scottish thrones, was called *Charles III* by [his supporters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacobitism).<sup>[\[409\]](#cite_note-CallMeGeorge-418)</sup>
